# Preset catalog — companion-module-disguise-liveupdate (Phase 1 design)

Design rules: [PRESET_CATALOG_RULES.md](PRESET_CATALOG_RULES.md). Source candidates with citations: [PHASE0_CANDIDATES.md](PHASE0_CANDIDATES.md). Every button row was drafted from Phase 0 candidates (column *source* lists the candidate ids), checked by an adversarial verifier against the candidate JSON and the rules, reviewed by a cross-category critic, and then merged by the lead (decisions listed at the end). Verifier corrections applied in place are marked ✎; rows the verifier still flagged carry ⚠ with the problem listed under the table. This file is rendered from `docs/research/phase1-catalog.json` by `scripts/render-catalog.mjs`; `scripts/gen-presets.mjs` turns the same JSON into `src/presetCatalog.ts`.

**Commands** (play, stop, section and track jumps, RenderStream workload control, failover) are not reachable through LiveUpdate; the module sends them over the Designer Session REST API as its 'Transport:', 'RenderStream:' and 'Failover:' actions (see *Commands* in the HELP). This catalog lists the LiveUpdate readouts and property knobs around them.

Status values: `doc-verified` (normal tier, shipped by default), `unverified` (Experimental tier: read-only, shown only when the connection setting *Show experimental presets* is on), `doc-verified` rows inside 99 Experimental are documented comprehensions kept there by rule. `live-verified` = the exact object/property pair returned a value from a Designer r34.0.3 Director on 2026-09-04 (Phase 3; evidence in `docs/research/live-verification.json` and `docs/research/live-verification-probes.json`). The *live* column shows the per-row result: `confirmed-value`, `confirmed-path-error` (subscription accepted, the Director reported an evaluation error, in every remaining case a project-specific one), `confirmed-no-value` (accepted, no value within the timeout), `not-run` (a selection had no value in the test project) or `n/a` (templates). Live-confirmed experimental rows were promoted to their home categories on 2026-09-04 (user decision; the JSON key `promotion` lists them).

Placing every normal-tier preset once creates one Director subscription per unique pair; presets that share a pair share the subscription (same variable name, same interval).

## Selection variables (module variables, set in the connection settings or with the Set selection action)

Presets cannot ship `$(custom:...)` references: Companion rewrites every `$(label:var)` in an imported preset to the connection label except `$(local:...)` (`Definitions.ts` → `replaceAllVariables`), so a shipped `$(custom:d3_track)` would arrive as `$(<connection>:d3_track)`. References to the module's own variables survive the rewrite, so the presets use the selection variables below. The host parses them before the feedback runs and re-evaluates the feedback when they change, which makes every preset built on a selection re-subscribe when the operator changes it. An unset selection is published as `$NA`, which the module never sends to the Director.

| selection variable | meaning | rows using it |
|---|---|---|
| `$(liveupdate:selBeat)` | a track beat as a number, e.g. 32 | 6 |
| `$(liveupdate:selDmxScreen)` | [Experimental] DMX screen name | 5 |
| `$(liveupdate:selEvDevice)` | [Experimental] Expression Variables device name (file name part of objects/ExpressionVariablesDevice/<name>.apx) | 13 |
| `$(liveupdate:selEvIndex)` | 0-based row index of the variable inside the device (unquoted integer) | 12 |
| `$(liveupdate:selEvLayer)` | [Experimental] Expression Variables layer name (module: prefix) | 1 |
| `$(liveupdate:selEvName)` | [Experimental] expression variable name, case-sensitive | 2 |
| `$(liveupdate:selEvUid)` | UID of the Expression Variables device, hex with 0x prefix | 7 |
| `$(liveupdate:selHost)` | hostname of the remote machine WITHOUT the :d3 suffix (the presets append it) | 10 |
| `$(liveupdate:selInstance)` | 0-based RenderStream instance index (unquoted integer) | 11 |
| `$(liveupdate:selLayer)` | layer name inside the selected track, e.g. Video 1 | 49 |
| `$(liveupdate:selLayerIndex)` | 0-based position in the track layer list, track.layers (unquoted integer), for the "(by index)" presets | 2 |
| `$(liveupdate:selLedScreen)` | LED screen name | 4 |
| `$(liveupdate:selMachine)` | Machine resource name as listed in d3Net Manager | 17 |
| `$(liveupdate:selProjector)` | projector name | 14 |
| `$(liveupdate:selRsLayer)` | 0-based index among the track's RenderStream leaf layers | 10 |
| `$(liveupdate:selScreen)` | Surface (screen2) name | 23 |
| `$(liveupdate:selScreenUid)` | UID of any display, hex with 0x prefix (right-click the editor title bar > Copy UID) | 14 |
| `$(liveupdate:selSection)` | 0-based section index (unquoted integer) | 5 |
| `$(liveupdate:selStageUid)` | Stage UID, hex with 0x prefix | 3 |
| `$(liveupdate:selTrack)` | track name as shown in Designer, e.g. Track 1 (quoted by the paths) | 84 |
| `$(liveupdate:selTransport)` | [Experimental] transport manager name other than default | 1 |
| `$(liveupdate:selWorkload)` | RenderStream workload id as unquoted decimal digits (uint64; the [EXP] RS Layer Workload ID preset shows it as text, or Cluster Workload widget > Copy UID / REST layerstatus) | 23 |

## Validation

- No id collisions, variable/pair conflicts, foreign text references, experimental writes or unknown candidates (checked at merge time).

## Write verification (Phase 3)

Three protocol-level runs (scripts/live-write-verify.mjs, groups neutral and output) and one module-level run (test/live-write.e2e.ts) against Designer r34.0.3. Every target was read first, changed by a small amount, confirmed through its own subscription, written back to the original value, confirmed again, and re-read after the run. The layer targets used track "demo", which is not the transport current track; the transport was stopped.

Date: 2026-09-04. Environment: {"track":"demo","layer":"Video 6","screen":"surface 1","projector":"projector 1","screenUid":"0x0123456789abcdef","currentTrack":"Show Track","playing":false}. Evidence: `docs/research/live-write-verification.json`, `docs/research/live-write-verification-output.json`, `docs/research/live-write-e2e.json`.

| run | result |
|---|---|
| neutral | {"write-accepted-and-restored":9} |
| output | {"write-accepted-and-restored":13} |
| e2e | {"brightness":{"written":true,"restored":true,"after":1},"layerEnabled":{"written":true,"restored":true,"after":true},"screenOffset":{"written":true,"restored":true,"after":"{\"x\":0,\"y\":3,\"z\":0}"}} |

The Director stores these properties as 32-bit floats: a written 0.9 reads back as 0.8999999761581421. The presets show the rounded value with toFixed().

Writable rows that were NOT exercised:

- ev_uid_float / ev_uid_float_up / ev_uid_float_down: the test project has no Expression Variables device
- fo_role, fo_targets, fo_hostname, fo_failover_timeout, fo_locked_to_director: failover topology, never written by this module (no write presets ship for them)
- monr_fps_expired, tr_transport_status, tr_track_length_beats, rs_layer_framerate: the API member has a setter but the catalog ships them as readouts only, so no write button exists

## Live verification (Phase 3)

Read-only: scripts/live-verify.mjs subscribed to every unique object/property pair of the catalog once (selections discovered from the running project), recorded the first value or error and unsubscribed; four follow-up probe runs tested replacement forms for the rows that failed. No `set` was sent; the writes were verified in a separate run (see writeVerification).

Environment: Designer r34.0.3 (rev 258249), project example-show, Director DIRECTOR01 (vx 4+), actor ACTOR01; selections: track "Show Track", layer "Colour Layer" (ColourAdjust), surface "surface 1", projector "projector 1", machine DIRECTOR01, host ACTOR01, workload 1000000000000000001 (RenderStream layer "RenderStream 2", no running instances). Date: 2026-09-04. Evidence: `docs/research/live-verification.json`, `docs/research/live-verification-probes.json`, `docs/research/live-verification-run2.json`, `docs/research/live-write-verification.json`.

| not-run | 7 | 20 |
| confirmed-path-error | 13 | 4 |
| confirmed-no-value | 0 | 1 |
| n/a | 12 | 0 |

## 01 Connection

> **Setup: selections & settings** (`conn_setup`, text preset): LiveUpdate can READ and SET Designer properties but cannot carry commands, so this module sends them over the Designer Session REST API on the same host and port: play, stop, section and track jumps, go to time / timecode / note / tag, brightness, volume, speed and engage are the 'Transport:' actions; starting, stopping, restarting and syncing RenderStream layers are the 'RenderStream:' actions; failing over and restoring a machine and the default routing are the 'Failover:' actions. RenderStream and Failover commands are destructive: they are refused until 'Allow destructive commands' is on in the connection settings, and then need two presses of the same button. Presets address show objects through the module's own selection variables ($(liveupdate:selTrack), $(liveupdate:selScreen),...). Set them once in the connection settings (section "Selections") or from a button with the "Set selection" action; every preset that uses a selection re-subscribes automatically when it changes. Normal-tier selections: selBeat = a track beat as a number, e.g. 32; selEvIndex = 0-based row index of the variable inside the device (unquoted integer); selEvUid = UID of the Expression Variables device, hex with 0x prefix; selHost = hostname of the remote machine WITHOUT the :d3 suffix (the presets append it); selInstance = 0-based RenderStream instance index (unquoted integer); selLayer = layer name inside the selected track (selTrack), e.g. Video 1; selLayerIndex = 0-based position in the track layer list (unquoted integer) for the "(by index)" presets; selLedScreen = LED screen name; selMachine = Machine resource name exactly as listed in d3Net Manager (the same string is used for remote node lookups and RenderStream machine names; confirm on your Director); selProjector = projector name; selRsLayer = 0-based index among the track's RenderStream leaf layers; selScreen = Surface (screen2) name; selScreenUid = UID of any display, hex with 0x prefix (right-click the editor title bar > Copy UID); selSection = 0-based section index (unquoted integer); selStageUid = Stage UID, hex with 0x prefix; selTrack = track name as shown in Designer, e.g. Track 1 (quoted by the paths); selWorkload = RenderStream workload id as unquoted decimal digits (the RS Layer Workload ID preset shows it as text; also Cluster Workload widget > Copy UID or REST GET /api/session/renderstream/layerstatus). Experimental presets (category 99, names prefixed [EXP]) are hidden until the connection setting "Show experimental presets" is on; they use: selDmxScreen = DMX screen name; selEvDevice = Expression Variables device name (file name part of objects/ExpressionVariablesDevice/<name>.apx); selEvLayer = Expression Variables layer name (module: prefix); selEvName = expression variable name, case-sensitive; selTransport = transport manager name other than default. Update intervals are prefilled from the connection settings (Monitoring / Playhead / State / Static) and can be edited per button.

|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

<details><summary>Notes, verifier corrections and flags</summary>

- `conn_session_name`: Category assignment (homeCategory 01 Connection, conn_ prefix) is outside the failover dimension's ownership; the lead must confirm the conn_ ids do not collide with rows drafted for 01 Connection and that the variable names sessionName/projectName/designerVersion are not used there for a different pair. Tests subsystem:SessionSystem resolution. Behaviour in solo mode (empty string?) unknown.
- `conn_project_name`: Same ownership caveat as conn_session_name (lead to confirm id and variableName uniqueness against 01 Connection). Global state on a documented subsystem anchor (the object is ignored). Project path is not exposed by a documented member; REST GET /api/session/status/project returns projectPath.
- `conn_designer_version`: Same ownership caveat as conn_session_name. Bare type names inside property expressions are documented (the LiveUpdate examples use VideoClip), so this is the strongest of the global rows. Live on r34.0.3 versionString() returned 'r34.0.3, rev 258249' and versionName() 'r34.0.3' (conn_designer_version_name); the module's own designer_version variable reads versionString() on every connection. Remote machines' versions are REST-only (GET /api/service/system/detectsystems).
- `conn_designer_version_name`: Sibling-derived from fo-38 (versionName(), d3.pyi:39304) rather than the candidate's own propertyPath; accepted, member is listed in the candidate JSON. Same ownership caveat as conn_session_name. Sibling static method listed in the fo-38 candidate (d3.pyi:39304).
- `conn_licence_days`: Same ownership caveat as conn_session_name. state.license (writable string) exists and is deliberately not catalogued. Value on a perpetual licence unknown.
- `conn_in_show`: Same ownership caveat as conn_session_name. Marginal; drop if the live test shows it never changes.
- `conn_status`: corrected sources, notes. notes understate the connectionState hazard: src/index.ts 'close' handler (lines 399-421) clears state and sets connection_status but never calls checkFeedbacks(); checkFeedbacks() runs only in handleMessage on subscriptions (line 556) and valuesChanged (line 654). Consequence: the button stays GREEN showing 'Disconnected' for the whole outage, and after (re)connect it stays red until the first Director message, which never arrives on a page that has no liveUpdateVariable feedback (subscribeFeedbacks() sends nothing). / sources should also cite src/index.ts (connection_status values, checkFeedbacks timing) and src/variables.ts (variable definition). / previewText 'Connected' with the base bgcolor FireBrick is contradictory in the preset browser; the generator should set previewStyle bgcolor to rgb(0,100,0) for the 'Connected' preview. / The openQuestions item about calling checkFeedbacks('connectionState') from the ws open/close handlers should be promoted to a proposed extension (it is required for this row to be trustworthy), not left as a question. Feedbacks: connectionState only (type boolean, options {}), with style override {bgcolor: rgb(0,100,0), color: rgb(220,220,220)} applied while instance.isConnectionReady() is true; the default button style (FireBrick) shows while disconnected. No liveUpdateVariable feedback on this row. Caveat (src/index.ts, INVENTORY §5): connectionState is re-evaluated only by checkFeedbacks(), which runs on 'subscriptions' and 'valuesChanged' messages, never from the ws open/close handlers. Therefore (a) after the socket closes the button stays GREEN with the text 'Disconnected' until the module reconnects and receives its first Director message, and (b) after a connect it stays FireBrick until a message arrives, which on a page without any liveUpdateVariable feedback may never happen (subscribeFeedbacks() sends nothing). The connection_status text is always correct because it is set directly in the open/close handlers. Until the proposed one-line extension (checkFeedbacks('connectionState') from the open/close handlers) is approved, HELP must tell operators to trust the text and keep at least one LiveUpdate readout on the same page. This is the only catalog row that uses connectionState.

</details>

## 02 Monitoring Local

> **Setup: local monitors** (`monl_setup`, text preset): Local monitors need no selection: every preset subscribes to subsystem:MonitoringManager.findLocalMonitor(...) on the Director Companion is connected to.  Shown: FPS (latest, 10-sample and 60-sample average), GPU profiler frame time (Total, Compositor, GUI, Render Everything, DMX, Video Upload, Capture Upload) in ms, CPU Total (unit stated as ms by the help site; live on r34.0.3 the graph reports yUnits = "Units", so treat the number as relative), GPU memory (MB), Designer process memory (MB) and the "Machine" health monitor percentages (CPU/GPU time, CPU/GPU memory, Disk; source: disguise's official Grafana dashboard; values confirmed live on r34.0.3 in the 0-100 range).  Also included after the r34.0.3 live run: Disk read/write MB/s (the developer guide calls Disk over LiveUpdate work in progress, but the values arrive), monitor and series name discovery, GPU thresholds, FPS min/max and graph categories.  Update rate: monitoring class (default 1000 ms). Never set 0: monitors push every frame. Colour rules assume a 60 Hz project: red when FPS falls 10 below (latest) or 5 below (averages), GPU Total >= 17 ms, Machine % >= 80.

| preset id | name | purpose | kind / actions | object path | property path | variable | value type / range | writable | update freq (class → default ms) and rationale | source | status | live (r34.0.3, 2026-09-04) | write |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `monl_fps` | FPS (latest) | Local frame rate, latest sample - the number in Designer's FPS bug; the primary health readout. | readout<br>colour: `{"operator":"<","value":50,"bgcolor":"rgb(178,34,34)"}` | `subsystem:MonitoringManager.findLocalMonitor("fps")` | `object.seriesAverage("Actual", 1)` | `fps` | number (float); 0..project refresh rate (e.g. 60). <details><summary>Notes, verifier corrections and flags</summary>

- `monl_fps`:  Same pair and variable name as the module's existing fpsMonitor preset, so upgrading keeps 'fps' stable. Red rule = guide's error level (10 fps below a 60 Hz target); adjust for 50/30 Hz projects. Research recommended 500 ms for this readout; the monitoring class default (1000 ms) is used - lower the class default if a faster bug is wanted.
- `monl_fps_avg10`:  The literal seriesAverage("Actual", 10) is the guide's recommendation applied to the documented signature; behaviour when nSamples exceeds the FIFO capacity is undocumented. Red rule = guide's warning level (5 fps below 60).
- `monl_fps_avg60`:  Evidence is disguise-one's official Grafana repo, not developer.disguise.one; usage string is exact.
- `monl_gpu_total`:  Guide example uses 100 ms for a graph; 1000 ms is enough for a button. Red threshold copied from the Grafana panel (17 ms).
- `monl_gpu_compositor`:  Series title exact from the official dashboard JSON. Help-page UI labels 'Frame' / 'Deliver Frames' are NOT series titles.
- `monl_gpu_gui`:  Series title exact from the official dashboard JSON.
- `monl_gpu_render_everything`:  Series title contains a space: "Render Everything".
- `monl_gpu_dmx`:  Usually sub-millisecond, hence two decimals.
- `monl_gpu_video_upload`:  Series title exact from the official dashboard JSON.
- `monl_gpu_capture_upload`:  Series title exact from the official dashboard JSON.
- `monl_cpu_total`:  UNIT OPEN: the guide heads this subscription 'CPU utilisation' but the help page defines the CPU graph in ms. No unit suffix and no colour rule until a live value confirms; the experimental row monl_cpu_units (graphDescriptor yUnits) answers it. Do not label as percent.
- `monl_gpu_mem`: corrected textTemplate, previewText. non-blocking: button title 'GPU mem' is also used by monl_machine_gpu_mem (percent); the MB suffix disambiguates at runtime but the preview browser shows two 'GPU mem' buttons Series title has NO space: "Usage(MB)" (contrast ProcessMemory). Research suggested 2000 ms; monitoring class (1000 ms) kept for uniformity - one cheap subscription. No colour rule: absolute figure depends on the GPU.
- `monl_proc_mem`:  Series title HAS a space: "Usage (MB)". UI labels 'Memory Used (MB)' / 'Delta (KB)' are not API titles.
- `monl_machine_cpu_time`:  GRAFANA-ONLY EVIDENCE: monitor name 'Machine' is not in the developer guide nor in the help page's Local graph list; it matches pyi class MachineMonitor(MetricMonitor). Confirm it resolves on r34.0.3 before shipping to the show page.
- `monl_machine_gpu_time`:  Grafana-only evidence, see monl_machine_cpu_time.
- `monl_machine_cpu_mem`:  Grafana-only evidence, see monl_machine_cpu_time.
- `monl_machine_gpu_mem`: corrected textTemplate, previewText. non-blocking: title 'GPU mem' duplicates monl_gpu_mem (MB); use 'GPU mem %' Grafana-only evidence, see monl_machine_cpu_time.
- `monl_machine_disk`:  Grafana-only evidence. The guide's 80-89 % warning / 90 %+ error rules for a future disk-utilisation metric may map to this series - confirm live.
- `monl_disk_read`:  CONFLICT: the developer guide says disk monitoring over LiveUpdate is 'in development (DSOF-20732)' while the official Grafana dashboard subscribes to exactly this series. Needs a live check on r34.0.3. Lead: moved to Experimental because the monitoring guide says Disk over LiveUpdate is still in development (DSOF-20732).
- `monl_disk_write`:  Same DSOF-20732 conflict as monl_disk_read. Lead: moved to Experimental because the monitoring guide says Disk over LiveUpdate is still in development (DSOF-20732).
- `monl_monitor_names`:  Inferred: MonitorCollection is a _BlipValue intermediate never shown traversed in a LiveUpdate expression. Comprehension syntax itself is documented. Discovery only - remove from show pages after use.
- `monl_cpu_series_names`:  Python 2.7: the argument must be False (capital F). graphDescriptor() may allocate a snapshot per evaluation - keep the static class (5000 ms).
- `monl_cpu_units`: non-blocking: object path is the doc-verified CPU monitor (mon-cpu-total) while the candidate mon-units example uses GPUMemory; the graphDescriptor(False).yUnits member is monitor-generic (GraphDescriptor, d3.pyi:18944-19015) and mon-series-names already applies graphDescriptor to findLocalMonitor("CPU"), so the rule (c) combination is accepted at experimental tier Monitor name substituted from the candidate's GPUMemory example onto the doc-verified CPU object path (mon-cpu-total). GraphDescriptor fields have pyi setters but act on a per-call snapshot - read-only, never set.
- `monl_gpu_mem_units`:  Sibling metadata (.xUnits,.graphTitle,.maxValue,.minData,.maxData) is available on the same snapshot if a units suffix or percent-of-max bar is wanted later.
- `monl_gpu_thresholds`:  May be an empty list for monitors without thresholds. Python 2.7 list-of-dict comprehension.
- `monl_fps_minmax`: non-blocking: textTemplate relies on Companion jsonpath(obj, path) returning the unwrapped scalar for '$.Actual.min' (Companion implements wrap:false, so a number is expected); the drafter already lists this for the 5.0.4 check Copies the whole buffer three times per evaluation - never faster than the monitoring class (1000 ms). Sample order assumed oldest-first ('in order'), so [-1] is newest - unverified. Button expression assumes Companion's jsonpath() returns the single matched value; verify on Companion 5.0.4.
- `monl_graph_categories`:  A property path that never references `object` is shown in no doc example; a successful read here would also unblock other dimensions' bare-type-name questions.

</details>

| preset id | textExpression | text | preview |
|---|---|---|---|
| `monl_fps` | yes | `` `FPS\n${isNumber($(liveupdate:fps)) ? toFixed($(liveupdate:fps), 1) : $(liveupdate:fps)}` `` | `FPS\n60.0` |
| `monl_fps_avg10` | yes | `` `FPS avg10\n${isNumber($(liveupdate:fpsAvg10)) ? toFixed($(liveupdate:fpsAvg10), 1) : $(liveupdate:fpsAvg10)}` `` | `FPS avg10\n59.8` |

## 03 Monitoring Remote

> **Setup: remote monitors** (`monr_setup`, text preset): Set the selection selHost to the hostname of the remote Designer machine WITHOUT the ":d3" suffix (the presets append it, e.g. selHost = ACTOR01 -> "ACTOR01:d3"). Hostname case sensitivity is undocumented: copy it exactly as shown in d3Net / the "Remote nodes list" preset.  Shown: remote FPS (latest and 10-sample average), whether that remote fps graph has expired (stopped receiving data) and the list of remote nodes the MonitoringManager knows (discovery aid for selHost).  Also included after the live run: remote Machine CPU/GPU time, GPU/CPU totals, GPU memory and the per-node monitor name list.  Open points: only "fps" is documented as a remote monitor name; whether the Director must first mark the remote graph in use (MonitoringManager.useMonitorByName) is untested. A wrong selHost produces a LiveUpdate error; the module backs off before retrying.

<details><summary>Notes, verifier corrections and flags</summary>

- `monr_fps`:  nodeName format '<hostname>:d3' (doc example 'IYPVM:d3'); selHost must be the bare hostname. Undocumented: hostname case sensitivity and whether the Director must first mark the remote graph in use (MonitoringManager.useMonitorByName). A wrong host triggers the subscribe-storm hazard until the module guard exists.
- `monr_fps_avg10`:  Rule (c) combination: doc-verified remote object path + doc-verified seriesAverage("Actual", 10); verifier confirms RemoteMonitor(MetricMonitor) at d3.pyi:39312 so the same accessor applies.
- `monr_fps_expired`: non-blocking: rule (c) combination of the doc-verified remote path with MetricMonitor.expired from mon-monitor-meta (whose own object path is the local fps monitor); supported by the verifier note (direct members on a doc-verified path qualify; RemoteMonitor(MetricMonitor) at d3.pyi:39312) and the researcher note naming object.expired as the remote liveness flag - accepted as doc-verified Rule (c) combination: doc-verified remote path + MetricMonitor.expired (verifier: direct members on a doc-verified path qualify; researcher note calls object.expired 'the best cheap is-this-remote-node-still-reporting flag'). expired has a pyi setter - NEVER set it. Interaction with MonitoringManager.preserveExpiredGraphs() untested.
- `monr_remote_nodes`:  Upgraded to doc-verified by the verifier (pyi member on a documented object path) but never shown through LiveUpdate in any doc. Whether the strings carry the ':d3' suffix is undocumented - the live test settles the selHost convention. Raw JSON shown; long lists will truncate on small keys.
- `monr_node_monitor_names`:  Same MonitorCollection _BlipValue caveat as monl_monitor_names. The $(liveupdate:selHost) substitution sits inside the property path here, not the object path.
- `monr_machine_cpu_time`: corrected textTemplate, previewText. non-blocking: title '<host> CPU' duplicates monr_cpu_total (ms); use 'CPU %' Only 'fps' is documented as a remote monitor name; whether nodes publish 'Machine' over d3Net is untested. Promote once monr_node_monitor_names lists it.
- `monr_machine_gpu_time`: corrected textTemplate, previewText. non-blocking: series title "GPU Time" substituted into the inferred mon-remote-any pattern (candidate example is "CPU Time"); sanctioned by the candidate description and by mon-machine-percent, experimental read-only - accepted, lead to confirm the rule (a)/(c) reading / non-blocking: title '<host> GPU' duplicates monr_gpu_total (ms); use 'GPU %' Series title substituted within the same documented 'Machine' monitor; remote availability untested.
- `monr_gpu_total`: corrected textTemplate, previewText. non-blocking: object path findRemoteMonitor("$(custom:d3_host):d3", "GPUProfiler") appears in no candidate; it substitutes the doc-verified monitor name from mon-gpuprofiler-total into the inferred mon-remote-any pattern whose description explicitly covers 'any documented local monitor name'. Accepted as experimental read-only; lead to confirm / non-blocking: title '<host> GPU' duplicates monr_machine_gpu_time (%); use 'GPU ms' Monitor name 'GPUProfiler' substituted into the inferred remote pattern; remote availability untested.
- `monr_cpu_total`: corrected textTemplate, previewText. non-blocking: object path findRemoteMonitor("$(custom:d3_host):d3", "CPU") appears in no candidate (same mon-remote-any substitution as monr_gpu_total); accepted as experimental read-only, lead to confirm / non-blocking: title '<host> CPU' duplicates monr_machine_cpu_time (%); use 'CPU total' (no unit, as the unit is open) Monitor name 'CPU' substituted into the inferred remote pattern; unit ambiguity inherited from the local row.
- `monr_gpu_mem`: non-blocking: object path findRemoteMonitor("$(custom:d3_host):d3", "GPUMemory") appears in no candidate (same mon-remote-any substitution as monr_gpu_total); accepted as experimental read-only, lead to confirm Monitor name 'GPUMemory' substituted into the inferred remote pattern; series title "Usage(MB)" has no space.

</details>

## 04 Transport State

> **Transport State: setup** (`tr_setup`, text preset): Object path for every preset in this category: transportManager:default (Designer's default transport manager; no selection needed). Readouts: playhead in beats (equals seconds at 60 bpm), play mode as string and as state enum, playing / stopped / holding flags, play-to-end and loop-section modes, track loaded, playback speed, current track name and length, section count, timecode chase and source status strings, incoming timecode and pre-roll countdown. Controls: master brightness and volume (readout, +/-5 % nudges with rotary support, FULL / ZERO / MUTE), ENGAGE / DISENGAGE and a one-button Engaged toggle (Toggle Disguise Boolean action). Values with state colours use the LiveUpdate Compare feedback. Transport commands (play, stop, section and track jumps, go to time / timecode / note / tag) cannot travel over LiveUpdate; this module sends them over the Session REST API as its 'Transport:' actions.  Also included after the live run: track position and timecode position strings, free-running flag, BPM at playhead, section index, current / next section names, section elapsed and remaining (beats), set-list track names, active layer count and the GUI playhead. Still experimental (99): the timecode source name and the playhead of a named transport (the test project has one transport and no timecode source).

|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

<details><summary>Notes, verifier corrections and flags</summary>

- `tr_playhead_beats`:  Units are beats, not seconds (beats == seconds only at 60 bpm); the seconds and timecode conversions are experimental rows (tr_trackposition, tr_timecodeposition). Module HELP subscribes this path at 100 ms; the playhead class default is 250 ms. tRender vs tCurrent semantics undocumented - compare with object.player.tCurrent (tr-02, skipped as duplicate) during the live test.
- `tr_playmode`:  Exact literal strings returned by PlayMode.__str__ are undocumented - capture them live before adding liveUpdateCompare rules; use tr_playmode_state (int enum) for colour feedbacks meanwhile. Not the same as object.play_mode (timecode-chase config, skipped).
- `tr_playmode_state`:  Numbering taken from the TrackPlayer constants; the mapping of constants to.state values is reasonable but unproven - confirm live. Different numbering from object.play_mode. Do not subscribe to object.player.playMode itself (untested _BlipValue JSON shape). Suggested extra compare rules: ==0 green (Play), ==4/==5 rgb(178,34,34) (holding).
- `tr_playing`:  No docstring in the stub; semantics from the member name. Text uses the Companion ternary operator - if unsupported on the target Companion, fall back to 'Playing\n$(liveupdate:isPlaying)'.
- `tr_stopped`:  No docstring in the stub. Complementary to tr_playing; keep both if you want a two-colour transport bar.
- `tr_holding`:  Link between the member and the help page's 'holding state' is inferred from the name but well supported.
- `tr_section_play_mode`:  Marginal - tr_playmode_state covers all modes in one variable; kept because a dedicated mode lamp is common on cue decks.
- `tr_loop_section_mode`:  Marginal - tr_playmode_state covers it.
- `tr_has_track`:  Accessing properties of a None field fails the expression (help guide); the module unsubscribes a path after 3 errors, so add track rows only once this is true.
- `tr_speed`:  Read-only (setting speed is TransportCommand.makeSetSpeed, no REST endpoint). Feature is off by default in Designer (enableTransportSpeedControl in Preview options).
- `tr_brightness`:  Shared pair with tr_brightness_up/down/full/zero. Compare rule mirrors the OSC module's 'brightness = 0' feedback.
- `tr_brightness_up`:  No clamp: a value above 1.0 is sent as-is (Designer behaviour untested). The module evaluates the Number value with new Function, so 'Math.min(1, $(liveupdate:brightness)+0.05)' would clamp if the lead engineer allows it. Float accumulation (0.15000000000000002) is harmless. Whether a LiveUpdate set equals TransportCommand.makeBrightness (propagation to actors, undo) is undocumented - REST POST /api/session/transport/brightness is the documented command path.
- `tr_brightness_down`:  No clamp: a value below 0.0 is sent as-is. Same set-semantics caveat as tr_brightness_up.
- `tr_brightness_full`:  Green = 'on'. Instant, not a fade (the OSC module's fade up/down have no LiveUpdate equivalent).
- `tr_brightness_zero`:  Red = 'off'. Instant blackout; pair with tr_brightness_full.
- `tr_volume`:  Shared pair with tr_volume_up/down/full/mute. Compare rule mirrors the OSC module's 'volume = 0' feedback.
- `tr_volume_up`:  No clamp above 1.0 (Designer behaviour untested; Math.min(1,...) is possible because the module evaluates the value with new Function). Set-semantics caveat as for brightness; REST POST /api/session/transport/volume is the documented command.
- `tr_volume_down`:  No clamp below 0.0.
- `tr_volume_full`:  Instant, not a fade.
- `tr_volume_mute`:  Instant mute; the previous level is not remembered (no unmute-to-previous without a selection variables).
- `tr_engaged`:  Mirrors REST GET activetransport 'engaged'. Shared pair with tr_engage / tr_disengage / tr_engaged_toggle.
- `tr_engage`:  Setter exists in the stub; REST POST /api/session/transport/engaged is the documented command path - verify the LiveUpdate set is honoured and propagated before relying on it.
- `tr_disengage`:  Disengaging silently ignores timecode / OSC / MIDI transport commands - label the button clearly.
- `tr_engaged_toggle`:  Requires the Toggle Disguise Boolean action module extension; until it ships, generate the two-step onOff fallback (tr_engage / tr_disengage).
- `tr_trackname`:  Fails while no track is loaded (see tr_has_track).
- `tr_section_count`:  Parentheses are required (method, not property). Combine with the experimental sectionIndex row for 'n / total'.
- `tr_transport_status`:  A setter exists in the stub (writable=yes) but this is a status readout - no set preset is generated. Closest LiveUpdate analogue to REST 'receivingTimecode'; the raw form is tr_tc_status.
- `tr_tc_status`:  No boolean TC-lock member exists in the stub. Live on r34.0.3, with no timecode source, the value was 'stopped' (the help example); the strings for a locked or chasing source are not recorded yet, so a lock lamp still needs them before a liveUpdateCompare rule can be written.
- `tr_preroll`:  Value outside a pre-roll is undocumented (probably 0).
- `tr_tc_incoming`:  Errors on projects with no timecode source (object.timecode is None; module unsubscribes after 3 path errors). Raw clock value, no adjustment applied. This is the incoming TC, not the track's TC position (see experimental tr_timecodeposition). Empty string while the transport has no timecode source (object.timecode is None); verified live in that state only.
- `tr_tc_source_status`:  Fails when object.timecode is None. Literal strings undocumented. Empty string while the transport has no timecode source (object.timecode is None); verified live in that state only.
- `tr_trackposition`:  Inferred: the OSC guide's currentBeat -> object.player.tRender substitution. secondsToTimestamp drops fractional seconds; use toFixed for tenths.
- `tr_timecodeposition`:  Inferred only in the currentBeat -> tRender substitution. Frame format follows the transport's SMPTE clock type.
- `tr_free_running`:  Member verified, meaning not: do not use as a TC-lock lamp until observed against a real timecode feed.
- `tr_bpm`:  Inferred currentBeat -> tRender substitution.
- `tr_section_index`:  Equivalent of the OSC transport's currentSection variable. Pair with tr_section_count for 'n / total'.
- `tr_current_section_name`:  Returns '' when the section has no note. Only the currentSection -> beatToSection(tRender) substitution is inferred.
- `tr_next_section_name`:  Behaviour of sectionToBeat past the last section is undocumented (may return '' or error - 3 errors unsubscribe the path). The verifier's safer form object.track.noteAtBeat(object.track.findBeatOfNextSection(object.player.tRender)) is not a Phase 0 candidate and was therefore not used.
- `tr_section_remaining`:  Beats, not seconds (equal at 60 bpm). The OSC guide's Timecode() helper is OSC-transport-local, hence plain arithmetic. Alternative with a documented end-of-track fallback (findBeatOfNextSection) is not a Phase 0 candidate.
- `tr_section_elapsed`:  Beats, not seconds.
- `tr_setlist_tracks`:  List comprehension (experimental tier by rule). Fails when object.setList is None. Individual entries: jsonpath(jsonparse($(liveupdate:setListTracks)), '$[0]').
- `tr_active_layer_count`:  len() over a returned list; consistent with the documented comprehension support but not shown in any doc.
- `tr_gui_playhead_beats`:  subsystem:GuiSystem is not shown in any doc and GuiSystem is described as GUI-local ('not for use by non-GUI systems'); may not resolve on a headless actor. Different pair from tr_playhead_beats, hence its own variable name.
- `tr_track_length_beats`:  Added at merge (critic: missing essential). Read-only use; lengthInBeats has a setter but changing the playing track length is not offered.

</details>

## 05 Track & Layer

> **Setup: Track & Layer** (`trk_setup`, text preset): Fill these selections in first (connection settings > Selections, or the "Set selection" action): selTrack = track name as shown in Designer (quoted automatically, e.g. Track 1); selLayer = layer name inside that track (Layer editor name, e.g. Video 1); selSection = 0-based section index for the section presets; selBeat = a track beat (float) for the note/beat presets; selLayerIndex = 0-based position in the track layer list for the "(by index)" presets. Readouts show: track name/length/BPM, section and layer counts, section start/end, note at beat, beat-to-time, cue count, TC adjust, and per-layer name / enabled / live / start / end / length / anchored plus the key-0 values of pos.x, pos.y, scale and brightness. Readouts are grey; the TC-adjust knob presets (amber) write to Designer. All values are track beats unless labelled time. Also included after the live run: layer name list, section table / note / length / start (s), section of a beat, cue tags at a beat, the cue table (beat, note, section flag), layer extents / crashed / dormant / fields / key times, patched brightness, brightness at a beat, all pos.x keys and the video key-0 resource. Still experimental (99): the text key (needs a Text layer).

<details><summary>Notes, verifier corrections and flags</summary>

- `trk_track_name`: Advisory: variableName trackName differs from the OSC-mirror id trackname only by case; keep only if the transport dimension owns trackname, otherwise operators will confuse the two. Resource.description on a Track returns the real name without underscore decoration. Left as trackName (not the OSC mirror trackname) because that id is expected to belong to the transport dimension's current-track pair.
- `trk_length_beats`: Advisory: writable 'no' although trk-lengthInBeats has a setter (row semantic = no set shipped); label 'Length' is shared with trk_length_sec and trk_layer_tlength. Writable in pyi but treated as configuration: no set preset. Doc says "(seconds)" but the guide says beats vs seconds is not a fixed relationship; label as beats.
- `trk_bpm`: Advisory: writable 'no' although trk-bpm has a setter (row semantic = no set shipped). BPM can vary along the track (bpmAt); this is the base value. Writable in pyi but a set is configuration, not shipped.
- `trk_section_start`:  sectionInfo(i) returns the end of the track when the index does not exist, so an out-of-range selSection degrades gracefully. An EMPTY selSection produces object.sectionInfo() and a path error (subscribe-storm hazard).
- `trk_note_at_beat`:  selBeat is a selection variable(s) introduced by this dimension (a float track beat). Exact-beat lookup: the beat must equal the cue beat.
- `trk_beat_to_sec`:  Uses the track tempo map (bpm may vary along the track).
- `trk_tc_adjust`:  Units not stated in pyi (UserName "TC Adjust"); assume seconds and verify live.
- `trk_tc_adjust_plus`:  Set rotaryActions:true on the preset options for the rotate steps. Track-level write placed in 05 (see openQuestions).
- `trk_layer_name`: Advisory: writable 'no' although lay-name has a setter (renaming deliberately not shipped). Object path chains SuperTrack.findLayerByName (pyi 48138) on the documented track: prefix in the documented chaining style; the exact call is not shown verbatim in any doc. A missing or duplicate layer name is undocumented (None -> path error). Layers use name, not description. Name has a setter but renaming is configuration: no set preset.
- `trk_layer_enabled`:  Shares the pair/variable with the 06 enable on/off/toggle presets. Object path chains SuperTrack.findLayerByName (pyi 48138) on the documented track: prefix in the documented chaining style; the exact call is not shown verbatim in any doc. A missing or duplicate layer name is undocumented (None -> path error).
- `trk_key_posx`:  Property path is doc-verbatim (on getLeafLayers()[0]); combined here with the findLayerByName object path. Only key 0 is read/written; if the field has more keyframes the animation continues from later keys. Keyframe times are track beats.
- `trk_key_posy`:  Field name "pos.y" is named on the LiveUpdate page alongside "pos.x" and "scale" (candidate key-posx-v description / openQuestions). Only key 0 is read/written; if the field has more keyframes the animation continues from later keys. Keyframe times are track beats.
- `trk_key_scale`:  The field is called "scale.x" on r34.0.3: findSequence("scale") returns None on every layer type tested (Video, ColourAdjust, Scroll), findSequence("scale.x") and findSequence("scale.y") return the sequence. Only key 0 is read/written; if the field has more keyframes the animation continues from later keys. Keyframe times are track beats.
- `trk_key_scaley`:  The field is called "scale.x" on r34.0.3: findSequence("scale") returns None on every layer type tested (Video, ColourAdjust, Scroll), findSequence("scale.x") and findSequence("scale.y") return the sequence. Only key 0 is read/written; if the field has more keyframes the animation continues from later keys. Keyframe times are track beats.
- `trk_key_brightness`:  Named keyBrightness so it cannot collide with the OSC-mirror brightness (transport master). Only key 0 is read/written; if the field has more keyframes the animation continues from later keys. Keyframe times are track beats.
- `trk_brightness_const`:  Shares the pair with the 06 "constant on/off" presets (guide recipe: set key 0 and disableSequencing = True for a constant value).
- `trk_layer_name_idx`: corrected liveTestPriority. liveTestPriority should be high: this is the doc-verbatim object path form and the probe that resolves the getLeafLayers() arity conflict (d3.pyi:48147) on which the fallback strategy for every layer row depends. Index i is the 0-based position in track.layers (the track layer list; the discovery preset "Layer names (list)" shows the same order). The doc-verbatim getLeafLayers()[i] object path fails on r34.0.3 with "Incorrect number of arguments to call" (d3.pyi:48147 declares getLeafLayers(type)); getLeafLayers(<Type>)[i] is not accepted as an object path either ("Name '…' not found"), only inside property expressions.
- `trk_key_posx_idx`: corrected liveTestPriority. liveTestPriority should be high: this is the exact object/property pair shown on the LiveUpdate page (the only fully doc-verbatim keyframe pair) and the reference test for the set path. Index i is the 0-based position in track.layers (the track layer list; the discovery preset "Layer names (list)" shows the same order). The doc-verbatim getLeafLayers()[i] object path fails on r34.0.3 with "Incorrect number of arguments to call" (d3.pyi:48147 declares getLeafLayers(type)); getLeafLayers(<Type>)[i] is not accepted as an object path either ("Name '…' not found"), only inside property expressions.
- `trk_layer_names`: corrected textTemplate. textTemplate uses `jsonparse(...).length` member access; replace with jsonpath '$.length'. / Advisory: doc-verified candidate placed in 99 Experimental with status doc-verified (consistent with the no-comprehension rule); lead to confirm experimental rows may carry status doc-verified. List order has no relationship to timeline position. Use jsonpath to pick one name. Doc-verified comprehension: experimental tier by rule, status kept.
- `trk_layer_tstart_bycomp`:  IndexError when no layer matches. Read-only: writing through a comprehension result is undocumented.
- `trk_section_table`: corrected textTemplate. textTemplate uses `jsonparse(...).length` member access; replace with jsonpath '$.length'. sectionLengthBeats takes a cue index per its docstring while sectionInfo uses a section index; verify on tracks with non-section cues.
- `trk_section_note`: designerVersion says r34.0.3 but the path calls Track.sectionInfo, which was added in r33.1 (same version note as trk_section_start / trk_section_start_sec_x). Composite taken from the inferred trk-sectionList comprehension (verifier: "reasonable inference"); formatNoteForBeat is an alternative.
- `trk_section_length`:  Docstring says the parameter is the CUE index of the section, not the 0-based section index; may disagree with sectionInfo on tracks with tag/note-only cues.
- `trk_section_of_beat`:  A "current section" preset needs the playhead beat from the transport dimension; do not feed a 250 ms variable into this path (each change re-subscribes).
- `trk_cue_tag_at_beat`:  Guard form taken from the candidate notes (tagAtBeat returns None when absent and the expressions guide says a None access fails). Tag.CUE == 1.
- `trk_tags_at_beat`: corrected textTemplate. textTemplate uses `jsonparse(...).length` member access; replace with jsonpath '$.length'.
- `trk_cue_table`: corrected textTemplate. textTemplate uses `jsonparse(...).length` member access; replace with jsonpath '$.length'. Walks every cue and chains through Cue resource refs; heavy, keep at 5 s. The nested tag comprehension ([... for t in cue.getTags()]) never delivered a value within the timeout on r34.0.3 although its pieces evaluate; use "Cue tags at beat" for tags.
- `trk_layer_fields`: corrected textTemplate. textTemplate uses `jsonparse(...).length` member access; replace with jsonpath '$.length'. Run once per project to find the field names for the keyframe presets, then remove.
- `trk_layer_keytimes`: corrected textTemplate. textTemplate uses `jsonparse(...).length` member access; replace with jsonpath '$.length'.
- `trk_key_posx_all`: corrected textTemplate. textTemplate uses `jsonparse(...).length` member access; replace with jsonpath '$.length'. / Advisory: doc-verified comprehension in 99 Experimental with status doc-verified; object path swapped from the doc's getLeafLayers()[0] to findLayerByName (allowed by rule c, but the row is no longer fully doc-verbatim, as the note says). Verbatim uses nKeys WITHOUT parentheses (contradicts pyi); if it fails try nKeys(). Doc uses getLeafLayers()[0] as the object path. Doc-verified comprehension: experimental tier by rule, status kept.
- `trk_key_video`:  Decorated field name "video" is inferred from the module property name (useful-strings addresses it by index).

</details>

## 06 Layer Control

> **Setup: Layer Control** (`lay_setup`, text preset): Requires selection variable(s) selTrack and selLayer (and selLayerIndex for the "(by index)" presets), see 05 Track & Layer. Presets: layer enable (green) / disable (red) / toggle, layer start and length nudges and sets (amber), keyframe key-0 nudges and sets for pos.x, pos.y, scale and brightness (amber, "(knob)" presets also drive rotary left/right when rotaryActions is on), brightness = 0 black, and brightness "constant" on/off (disableSequencing). Each control also subscribes to its own value so the button shows the live number. Anchored layers reject start/length changes; only key 0 is changed, later keyframes still play. Sets are persistent and undoable in Designer.

<details><summary>Notes, verifier corrections and flags</summary>

- `lay_enable_on`:  Set via LiveUpdate is not shown verbatim (only key.v is) but the setter exists and the doc says sets are undoable and persistent. Object path chains SuperTrack.findLayerByName (pyi 48138) on the documented track: prefix in the documented chaining style; the exact call is not shown verbatim in any doc. A missing or duplicate layer name is undocumented (None -> path error).
- `lay_enable_toggle`: Advisory: the setToDisguiseToggle action carries only variableName (no value option); acceptable for the proposed extension but the generator must emit the fallbackIds lay_enable_on/lay_enable_off until the action exists. Requires the Toggle Disguise Boolean action action; until it exists the generator should emit lay_enable_on / lay_enable_off instead.
- `lay_tstart_plus`:  Anchored layers "cannot have their start time moved or have their extents changed" (d3.pyi:47805): the set may be rejected; check trk_layer_anchored. Set rotaryActions:true for the rotate steps.
- `lay_tstart_minus`:  Anchored layers "cannot have their start time moved or have their extents changed" (d3.pyi:47805): the set may be rejected; check trk_layer_anchored.
- `lay_tlength_plus`:  Anchored layers "cannot have their start time moved or have their extents changed" (d3.pyi:47805): the set may be rejected; check trk_layer_anchored. Set rotaryActions:true for the rotate steps.
- `lay_tlength_minus`:  Anchored layers "cannot have their start time moved or have their extents changed" (d3.pyi:47805): the set may be rejected; check trk_layer_anchored.
- `lay_posx_plus`:  Doc-verbatim property path (set example "sets the first X-position keyframe to 42.5"). Step 1 assumes Designer pos units; adjust after a live test. Set rotaryActions:true for the rotate steps. Only key 0 is read/written; if the field has more keyframes the animation continues from later keys. Keyframe times are track beats.
- `lay_posx_minus`:  Doc-verbatim property path (set example "sets the first X-position keyframe to 42.5"). Step 1 assumes Designer pos units; adjust after a live test. Only key 0 is read/written; if the field has more keyframes the animation continues from later keys. Keyframe times are track beats.
- `lay_posx_zero`:  Doc-verbatim property path (set example "sets the first X-position keyframe to 42.5"). Step 1 assumes Designer pos units; adjust after a live test. Only key 0 is read/written; if the field has more keyframes the animation continues from later keys. Keyframe times are track beats.
- `lay_posy_plus`:  Field "pos.y" named on the LiveUpdate page next to "pos.x". Set rotaryActions:true for the rotate steps. Only key 0 is read/written; if the field has more keyframes the animation continues from later keys. Keyframe times are track beats.
- `lay_posy_minus`:  Field "pos.y" named on the LiveUpdate page next to "pos.x". Only key 0 is read/written; if the field has more keyframes the animation continues from later keys. Keyframe times are track beats.
- `lay_posy_zero`:  Field "pos.y" named on the LiveUpdate page next to "pos.x". Only key 0 is read/written; if the field has more keyframes the animation continues from later keys. Keyframe times are track beats.
- `lay_scale_plus`:  The field is called "scale.x" on r34.0.3: findSequence("scale") returns None on every layer type tested (Video, ColourAdjust, Scroll), findSequence("scale.x") and findSequence("scale.y") return the sequence. Only key 0 is read/written; if the field has more keyframes the animation continues from later keys. Keyframe times are track beats.
- `lay_scaley_plus`:  The field is called "scale.x" on r34.0.3: findSequence("scale") returns None on every layer type tested (Video, ColourAdjust, Scroll), findSequence("scale.x") and findSequence("scale.y") return the sequence. Only key 0 is read/written; if the field has more keyframes the animation continues from later keys. Keyframe times are track beats.
- `lay_scale_minus`:  The field is called "scale.x" on r34.0.3: findSequence("scale") returns None on every layer type tested (Video, ColourAdjust, Scroll), findSequence("scale.x") and findSequence("scale.y") return the sequence. Only key 0 is read/written; if the field has more keyframes the animation continues from later keys. Keyframe times are track beats.
- `lay_scaley_minus`:  The field is called "scale.x" on r34.0.3: findSequence("scale") returns None on every layer type tested (Video, ColourAdjust, Scroll), findSequence("scale.x") and findSequence("scale.y") return the sequence. Only key 0 is read/written; if the field has more keyframes the animation continues from later keys. Keyframe times are track beats.
- `lay_scale_one`:  The field is called "scale.x" on r34.0.3: findSequence("scale") returns None on every layer type tested (Video, ColourAdjust, Scroll), findSequence("scale.x") and findSequence("scale.y") return the sequence. Only key 0 is read/written; if the field has more keyframes the animation continues from later keys. Keyframe times are track beats.

Only key 0 is read/written; if the field has more keyframes the animation continues from later keys. Keyframe times are track beats.
- `lay_bright_plus`:  Range not stated in pyi (guide example sets 25.0); step 0.1 assumes 0..1. Set rotaryActions:true for the rotate steps. Only key 0 is read/written; if the field has more keyframes the animation continues from later keys. Keyframe times are track beats.
- `lay_bright_minus`:  Range not stated in pyi (guide example sets 25.0); step 0.1 assumes 0..1. Only key 0 is read/written; if the field has more keyframes the animation continues from later keys. Keyframe times are track beats.
- `lay_bright_full`:  Range not stated in pyi (guide example sets 25.0); step 0.1 assumes 0..1. Only key 0 is read/written; if the field has more keyframes the animation continues from later keys. Keyframe times are track beats.
- `lay_bright_zero`:  Module.brightness docstring: zero brightness means fully transparent. Only key 0 is read/written; if the field has more keyframes the animation continues from later keys. Keyframe times are track beats.
- `lay_bright_const_on`:  Setter d3.pyi:17126; useful-strings shows layer.fields[field].disableSequencing = False. Not in the lead's explicit 06 list; added because it completes the brightness-knob recipe (see openQuestions).

## 07 Stage & Screen

> **Stage & Screen setup** (`stg_setup`, text preset): Fill these selections in before dropping the presets (connection settings > Selections, or the "Set selection" action): selScreen = exact Designer name of the Surface (Screen2), e.g. Surface 1 (the presets add the quotes); selProjector = projector name, e.g. projector 1; selScreenUid = UID of any display copied from its editor title bar (right-click > Copy UID), including the 0x prefix - the "Display (UID)" presets work for LED and DMX screens too. Readouts: surface name, mesh and resolution, health flags, offset and rotation, master fade, hold output, render layer (on/off stage), the projectors of a surface and the surfaces of a projector; projector name, resolution and master fade. Controls (amber = write, green = on/enable, red = off/hold): offset X/Y/Z nudges and reset, master fade nudges and 1.0 / 0.0, hold ON/OFF/toggle, ON/OFF stage - for the surface, for the projector and for any display addressed by UID. Sets are undoable in Designer and persist in the project. Nudge presets also respond to rotary encoders (right = +, left = -). Also included after the live run: the projectors of a surface, the surfaces of a projector, LED screen name / fade / hold / offset (selection selLedScreen = LED screen name) and stage display count / dynamic blend (selection selStageUid = Stage UID, hex with 0x prefix). Still experimental (99): DMX screen rows (no DMX screen in the test project) and the stage venue name (no venue assigned).

<details><summary>Notes, verifier corrections and flags</summary>

- `stg_screen_name`:  Shows ERROR / PATH_ERROR when the name is wrong; use it as the first sanity check.
- `stg_screen_offset_reset`:  Destructive for a positioned surface; the set is undoable in Designer. A single-axis reset is {"x": 0.0} (documented partial set).
- `stg_screen_offset_x`:  Read path documented (projector:projector_1.offset.x). A scalar set via LiveUpdate is undocumented (candidate writable = unknown) although the shipped v1.0.2 presets already use it; documented fallback is a partial JSON set {"x": n} on object.offset. Live-test first.
- `stg_screen_offset_x_up`: corrected notes. setToDisguiseNumber writes to object.offset.x, whose candidate stage-003 is writable=unknown; the verifier note states no doc shows a LiveUpdate set on a scalar sub-path (only Vec.x has a Python setter). Candidate status is doc-verified so the row is not an 'inferred write' by status, but the write itself is undocumented and must be gated: withhold from the shipped library until a live Director test confirms the scalar set. The documented alternative (partial JSON set {"x": n} on object.offset) cannot express a nudge because setToDisguiseJSON performs no arithmetic (src/actions.ts processJSONValue only JSON.parses after variable substitution). Lead decision: shipped in the normal tier. The Vec.x field has a Python setter (rules §1) and the v1.0.2 module already shipped the same "Screen X +1 / -1" write on object.offset.x, so removing it would regress existing users. A scalar set on object.offset.x is not shown in the LiveUpdate documentation (the documented form is a partial JSON set on object.offset, see stg_screen_offset_reset); confirm on a Director before relying on it (live-test priority high). If the scalar set is rejected, replace the action with a JSON set {"x": <value>} on object.offset.
- `stg_screen_offset_x_down`: corrected notes. Same as stg_screen_offset_x_up: write to a writable=unknown scalar sub-path (stage-003); gate on live test. Lead decision: shipped in the normal tier. The Vec.x field has a Python setter (rules §1) and the v1.0.2 module already shipped the same "Screen X +1 / -1" write on object.offset.x, so removing it would regress existing users. A scalar set on object.offset.x is not shown in the LiveUpdate documentation (the documented form is a partial JSON set on object.offset, see stg_screen_offset_reset); confirm on a Director before relying on it (live-test priority high). If the scalar set is rejected, replace the action with a JSON set {"x": <value>} on object.offset.
- `stg_screen_rotation`:  Readout only; a rotation write is possible via partial JSON on the same pair but is not shipped as a preset.
- `stg_screen_fade`:  Amber compare rule flags a dimmed display.
- `stg_screen_fade_up`:  No clamping in the expression; Designer is expected to clamp to 0..1 (unverified).
- `stg_screen_fade_zero`:  Show-critical: verify the fade is restored by the 1.0 preset.
- `stg_screen_hold_toggle`:  Fallback: use the ON/OFF pair until setToDisguiseToggle exists.
- `stg_screen_on_stage`:  Restores plain On stage; surfaces that were on an AR/MR layer (2-7) lose that assignment.
- `stg_screen_off_stage`:  Show-critical; pair with stg_screen_on_stage.
- `stg_screen_tracking_live`:  Pair with stg_screen_has_tracking to tell untracked from tracking-lost.
- `stg_proj_name`:  Quoted-name form documented; older Designer versions need the sanitised name (projector_1).
- `stg_proj_in_error`:  Documented projector: path combined with the Resource.isInError member (Projector is a Resource; stage-035 verifier note).
- `stg_proj_has_feed`:  No docstring; meaning inferred from the name.
- `stg_proj_fade`:  Amber compare rule flags a dimmed display. Display.master_fade on the documented projector: path (stage-007 notes: the property lives on Display, so Projector has it).
- `stg_proj_fade_up`:  No clamping in the expression; Designer is expected to clamp to 0..1 (unverified). Display.master_fade on the documented projector: path (stage-007 notes: the property lives on Display, so Projector has it).
- `stg_proj_fade_down`:  Display.master_fade on the documented projector: path (stage-007 notes: the property lives on Display, so Projector has it).
- `stg_proj_fade_full`:  Display.master_fade on the documented projector: path (stage-007 notes: the property lives on Display, so Projector has it).
- `stg_proj_fade_zero`:  Show-critical: verify the fade is restored by the 1.0 preset. Display.master_fade on the documented projector: path (stage-007 notes: the property lives on Display, so Projector has it).
- `stg_proj_hold`:  Display.holdOutput on the documented projector: path (stage-007/008 notes + stage-035 verifier: Projector(SlcAwareDisplay) inherits Display).
- `stg_proj_hold_on`:  Display.holdOutput on the documented projector: path (stage-007/008 notes + stage-035 verifier: Projector(SlcAwareDisplay) inherits Display).
- `stg_proj_hold_off`:  Display.holdOutput on the documented projector: path (stage-007/008 notes + stage-035 verifier: Projector(SlcAwareDisplay) inherits Display).
- `stg_proj_hold_toggle`:  Fallback: use the ON/OFF pair until setToDisguiseToggle exists. Display.holdOutput on the documented projector: path (stage-007/008 notes + stage-035 verifier: Projector(SlcAwareDisplay) inherits Display).
- `stg_uid_name`:  Rename-proof getByUID path (documented LiveUpdate object path); works for Surface, LED, DMX and projector alike, so this is the normal-tier route to LED/DMX screens until the ledscreen:/dmxscreen: prefixes are confirmed. Store the UID with its 0x prefix in $(liveupdate:selScreenUid) (right-click the screen editor title bar > Copy UID).
- `stg_uid_fade`:  Amber compare rule flags a dimmed display. Rename-proof getByUID path (documented LiveUpdate object path); works for Surface, LED, DMX and projector alike, so this is the normal-tier route to LED/DMX screens until the ledscreen:/dmxscreen: prefixes are confirmed. Store the UID with its 0x prefix in $(liveupdate:selScreenUid) (right-click the screen editor title bar > Copy UID).
- `stg_uid_fade_up`:  No clamping in the expression; Designer is expected to clamp to 0..1 (unverified). Rename-proof getByUID path; store the UID with its 0x prefix in $(liveupdate:selScreenUid).
- `stg_uid_fade_down`:  Rename-proof getByUID path; store the UID with its 0x prefix in $(liveupdate:selScreenUid).
- `stg_uid_fade_full`:  Rename-proof getByUID path; store the UID with its 0x prefix in $(liveupdate:selScreenUid).
- `stg_uid_fade_zero`:  Show-critical: verify the fade is restored by the 1.0 preset. Rename-proof getByUID path; store the UID with its 0x prefix in $(liveupdate:selScreenUid).
- `stg_uid_hold`:  Display.holdOutput on the documented getByUID path (stage-055 notes: UID works for every display type). Store the UID with its 0x prefix in $(liveupdate:selScreenUid).
- `stg_uid_hold_on`:  Display.holdOutput on the documented getByUID path; store the UID with its 0x prefix in $(liveupdate:selScreenUid).
- `stg_uid_hold_off`:  Display.holdOutput on the documented getByUID path; store the UID with its 0x prefix in $(liveupdate:selScreenUid).
- `stg_uid_hold_toggle`:  Fallback: use the ON/OFF pair until setToDisguiseToggle exists. Display.holdOutput on the documented getByUID path; store the UID with its 0x prefix in $(liveupdate:selScreenUid).
- `stg_screen_projectors`:  Candidate is doc-verified, but list comprehensions are excluded from the normal tier by the design rules, so it ships read-only here. Doc-verified comprehension: experimental tier by rule, status kept.
- `stg_proj_screens`:  Candidate is doc-verified, but list comprehensions are excluded from the normal tier by the design rules, so it ships read-only here. Doc-verified comprehension: experimental tier by rule, status kept.
- `stg_led_name`:  Resource.description on the inferred ledscreen: path (stage-029 notes: all Display/Object/Resource members apply to LedScreen). Prefix ledscreen: follows the documented type:name rule but appears in no fetched page (verifier grep count 0); confirm by Alt+dragging the LED screen from its editor into an expression field. Use the Display (UID) presets for control.
- `stg_led_fade`:  Write excluded (inferred). Prefix ledscreen: follows the documented type:name rule but appears in no fetched page (verifier grep count 0); confirm by Alt+dragging the LED screen from its editor into an expression field. Use the Display (UID) presets for control.
- `stg_led_hold`:  Write excluded (inferred). Prefix ledscreen: follows the documented type:name rule but appears in no fetched page (verifier grep count 0); confirm by Alt+dragging the LED screen from its editor into an expression field. Use the Display (UID) presets for control.
- `stg_led_offset`:  Write excluded (inferred). Prefix ledscreen: follows the documented type:name rule but appears in no fetched page (verifier grep count 0); confirm by Alt+dragging the LED screen from its editor into an expression field. Use the Display (UID) presets for control.
- `stg_stage_displays`:  No stage: prefix is documented; the Stage is reached by getByUID with the UID from the Stage editor title bar stored (with 0x prefix) in $(liveupdate:selStageUid). Whether the Stage editor offers Copy UID is an open question.
- `stg_stage_dyn_blend`:  Write excluded (inferred). No stage: prefix is documented; the Stage is reached by getByUID with the UID from the Stage editor title bar stored (with 0x prefix) in $(liveupdate:selStageUid). Whether the Stage editor offers Copy UID is an open question.
- `stg_uid_render_layer`:  Added at merge: the UID route is the only normal-tier path to an LED/DMX wall.
- `stg_uid_on_stage`:  Added at merge.
- `stg_uid_off_stage`:  Added at merge.
- `stg_uid_in_error`:  Added at merge.
- `stg_proj_active_stage`:  Added at merge.

</details>

## 08 Expression Variables

> **EV setup: custom variables** (`ev_setup`, text preset): Expression Variables device readouts. Fill these selections in first (connection settings > Selections, or the "Set selection" action): selEvUid = the device UID as an unquoted hex literal (Designer: right-click the Expression Variables device editor title bar > Copy UID, e.g. 0x0123456789abcdef); selEvIndex = zero-based row index of the variable in the device editor (plain integer, no quotes; rows can be drag-reordered so re-check after edits). Presets in this category read the i-th variable through getByUID(selEvUid).container.variables[selEvIndex]: defaultFloat (value of a Float variable), defaultString (value of a String variable), name and type (0 Float, 1 String, 2 Function). The readouts are read-only. Two knobs (EV float +/-0.1) write defaultFloat through the list subscript; that write is the one part of this category that could not be tested on the verification rig, because the test project had no Expression Variables device. Try them on a spare variable before using them in a show. Experimental rows (see 99 Experimental) additionally need selEvDevice (device name = filename part of objects/ExpressionVariablesDevice/<name>.apx), selEvName (variable name, case-sensitive) and selEvLayer (name of an ExpressionVariables layer currently under the playhead). None of the Expression Variables rows could be checked live: the test project has no Expression Variables device.

<details><summary>Notes, verifier corrections and flags</summary>

- `ev_uid_float`: note: candidate ev-device-by-uid is doc-verified with writable=yes; the row is read-only by drafter choice (verifier caveat: set through a list subscript is undocumented). Acceptable, but see missingRows for the nudge pair the lead may want. Read-only by design: pyi has a defaultFloat setter (d3.pyi:16052) and the official useful-strings snippet writes var.defaultFloat, but no doc shows a LiveUpdate set through a list subscript (only.sequence.key(0).v). After the live test this row becomes the nudge knob (setToDisguiseNumber '$(liveupdate:evFloat)+<step>'). Whether defaultFloat is the LIVE value for device variables (pyi UserName 'Default') also needs confirming. selEvUid must be an unquoted 0x hex literal; selEvIndex a plain integer. IndexError if the index is out of range.
- `ev_uid_string`: note: rule (c) combination - object path from ev-device-by-uid (doc-verified), member defaultString from ev-string-by-index whose inferred status is due solely to the undocumented prefix (verifier: 'defaultString at 16056 with setter at 16064 confirmed ... Prefix undocumented'). Accepted by parity with the candidate's own defaultFloat chain; lead to confirm parity counts as verifier support. Object path from ev-device-by-uid (doc-verified getByUID), property chain container -> variables -> defaultString verified in pyi (16056/16064). For Function-type variables the expression text may also live here (unconfirmed). Read-only until the subscript-set live test; would become setValue with setToDisguiseString.
- `ev_uid_name`: note: same rule (c) parity combination as ev_uid_string (name getter 16075 confirmed in ev-name-by-index; prefix is the only inferred element). pyi has a name setter (16083) but renaming breaks every expression that uses the variable, so no write is ever exposed. Use as the first live-test subscription for the UID path.
- `ev_uid_type`: note: same rule (c) parity combination (type 16087 + Options confirmed in ev-type-by-index). Ternary in textTemplate is a supported Companion expression operator; the plain-text fallback in notes is fine. Enum mapping from d3.pyi:16091 Options {0:'Float',1:'String',2:'Function'}. pyi setter exists but changing a variable's type is not an operator action; never exposed. Uses the Companion expression ternary operator; if the generator's validator rejects it, fall back to plain 'Type\n$(liveupdate:evType)'.
- `ev_uid_description`:  Added at merge. description is the Resource base member (d3.pyi:40438).
- `ev_uid_float_up`:  Added at merge. defaultFloat has a Python setter (d3.pyi:16052) but a LiveUpdate set through a list subscript is undocumented; if the live test fails these two rows are removed (no experimental writes).
- `ev_uid_float_down`:  Added at merge; see ev_uid_float_up.

</details>

## 09 RenderStream

> **RenderStream setup** (`rs_setup`, text preset): RenderStream presets read subsystem:RenderStreamSystem (cluster/instance health, per-node flags, receive statuses, active latency) and Machine takeover. Fill these selections in first (connection settings > Selections, or the "Set selection" action): selWorkload = workload ID as a Python int - copy it from the Cluster Workload widget ('Copy UID') or from REST GET /api/session/renderstream/layers then GET /api/session/renderstream/layerstatus?uid=<layer uid> (workload.uid); if the copied value is hex, enter it as 0x...; selInstance = zero-based instance index inside the workload (0 = first render node); selMachine = render node / machine name exactly as shown in the Cluster Workload widget Instances > Machine column. The 'RS workload layer count' preset shows how many layers carry a workload (on r34.0.3 the id map itself serialises empty, so read ids through the RS Layer Workload ID preset). The layer-route presets (RS Layer ...) need selTrack (track name) and selRsLayer (zero-based index of the RenderStream layer among the track's RenderStream leaf layers); the RS Layer Workload ID preset shows the workload id as text ready for selWorkload. The aggregate rows (node summary, running count, any dropping / down, health list, min FPS, max latency, dropped total, all subscribed, last errors, status messages) were verified live with a workload that had no running instances. All RenderStream rows are read-only: LiveUpdate cannot start or stop a workload. The module's 'RenderStream:' actions (start, stop, restart and sync layers) send those commands over the Session REST API; they are destructive, so they need 'Allow destructive commands' in the connection settings and two presses. While a workload has no running instances the per-instance rows show PATH_ERROR (the Director answers "out of range error accessing workload instance"); the module backs off and retries on its own.

<details><summary>Notes, verifier corrections and flags</summary>

- `rs_enabled`:  No setter in pyi. Useful first preset to prove the subsystem object path resolves before adding workload rows.
- `rs_licensing`:  Marginal: only one enum value is named. Capture other codes live before relying on the red colour rule.
- `rs_active_latency`:  Units (frames vs seconds) undocumented; compare with the experimental per-stream max latency on a live system before adding a threshold colour.
- `rs_workload_layers`:  The JSON keys are the workload ids. Whether the pyi 'map' _BlipValue serialises as a dictionary is undocumented, and uint64 ids may lose precision if emitted as JSON numbers - verify live. Raw JSON is shown because the keys, not a scalar, are the payload. getWorkloadLayers() is a map<uint64, Layer>; on r34.0.3 the LiveUpdate serialiser emits {} for it and iterating its keys fails with "long too big to convert". len() works. Read workload ids through the RS Layer Workload ID preset.
- `rs_workload_instances`:  Text expression assumes the struct serialises with the pyi property names as JSON keys (undocumented). Behaviour for a stopped workload ([] vs error) is undocumented.
- `rs_workload_receive`:  Counters advance every frame, so the 1 s monitoring throttle is mandatory. The experimental comprehension rows derive min fps / max latency / dropped packets from this list.
- `rs_stream_receive`:  machineName must be a quoted Python str; selMachine must match the Cluster Workload widget Machine column exactly. This form is only in the Live Update page, not the monitoring guide.
- `rs_inst_machine`: corrected textTemplate, previewText, notes. textTemplate 'Instance $(custom:d3_instance)\n$(liveupdate:rsInstanceMachineName)' embeds a custom variable in preset text. Companion rewrites every $(label:var) reference in preset text to the connection's label except local/internal (report-companion_api.md:124: REPLACE_VARIABLE_REGEX with allowedSet {'local'}), so the placed button would read $(liveupdate:d3_instance), which is undefined. Only $(liveupdate:<variableName>) may appear in button text. Setter exists in pyi but the value is a transient struct; no set preset. Do not reference $(custom:...) in button text: the host rewrites non-local variable prefixes in preset text to the connection label.
- `rs_inst_dropping`:  Flag can toggle rapidly during a stutter; the 500 ms state throttle limits flicker (candidate suggested 1000 ms).
- `rs_inst_status`:  Ship for live-capture of the enum values; no colour rule until codes are mapped.
- `rs_inst_health_msg`:  Same text as REST layerstatus instances[].healthMessage.
- `rs_inst_health_status`:  Also.priority and.details exist on WorkloadHealth; not shipped as separate rows.
- `rs_nodes_summary`:  Python 2.7-safe. Long strings overflow a 72 px button; prefer one button per instance for large clusters.
- `rs_running_count`:  Calls getWorkloadInstances twice per evaluation; fine at the throttle. %-formatting is Python 2.7-safe.
- `rs_any_down`:  Verifier caveat: any([]) is False, so a stopped workload with an empty instance list reads OK. Pair with rs_exp_running_count, or rewrite as 'not all([...])' if stopped must read as down.
- `rs_health_list`:  The doc says health is computed and must be subscribed per instance, so change notification for this comprehension is doubtful; test whether it updates when health changes. The documented per-instance rows are the shipped form.
- `rs_min_fps`:  'or [0.0]' avoids min() of an empty list raising. No stateColour shipped because the alarm threshold depends on the show frame rate; operators can add liveUpdateCompare '<' <fps>.
- `rs_max_latency`:  Compare with rs_active_latency on a live system to establish units.
- `rs_dropped_total`: corrected color. color is rgb(220,70,90), which is not the fixed readout foreground rgb(220,220,220); experimental rows change only the background to rgb(70,70,90). Counter never resets while the workload runs; a rising value, not the absolute number, is the alarm.
- `rs_all_subscribed`:  all([]) is True when no streams are wanted.
- `rs_last_errors`:  Candidate suggested 2000 ms; strings fall in the 500 ms state class, which is harmless here.
- `rs_status_msgs`:  Meaning of the ignoreDedicatedDirectorStatus argument (False) is undocumented.
- `rs_layer_workload_id`:  Verifier: a class-name argument inside the object expression is not demonstrated in docs (only no-arg getLeafLayers()[0]); if it fails, try track:"..".getLeafLayers()[n] with a plain index. Value when the workload is stopped unknown (possibly 0). This row also proves the layer route for the other layer rows. str() keeps the 64-bit id exact: as a JSON number it loses precision in Companion (1000000000000000001 arrived as 1000000000000000000).
- `rs_layer_health`:  workloadHealth is a property (not a method), so the doc's 'computed property must be accessed per-instance' caveat may not apply; if it pushes updates this is the simplest whole-cluster health button.
- `rs_layer_pool_online`:  pool exists only on RenderStreamModuleConfig; a RenderStream Local layer (RenderStreamLocalModuleConfig) has no pool and this row errors there.
- `rs_layer_asset_errors`:  Same RenderStreamLocalModuleConfig caveat as rs_exp_layer_pool_online.
- `rs_layer_framerate`:  Inferred write excluded by rule (no set action). Verifier: REST layerconfig only allows divisors 1, 2, 3, and whether a running workload applies a change without reconfigureWorkload() is undocumented - revisit as a setValue row only after a live test.

</details>
